Lawyer Bicer, 17 years old, H. E. with K. S. And 16 - year - old E. T. He met with her, and based on the children's narrative, he wrote the following: "Two of the three children who were seen were exposed to a naked search before they were put in the ring for hospital transport. One of the kids said that in June they were taken into a room without a hospital camera, a guard told them to undress, and when they refused, four or five guards entered the room, forced the children to take their clothes off. Another kid told the hospital dispatcher that on a single day, four guards forced their clothes off and searched naked. The juvenile prisoners stated that the other juvenile prisoners were subjected to harsh beatings and severe insults in ways that others could see and hear directly. They told me they saw other children getting beaten up. Toilets in the wards are locked in automatic doors all day long, and only guards can open the doors. Therefore, the guardian's permission to go to the bathroom in the wards. There are bathrooms and bathrooms in each room. With the morning census at 7:00 a.m., the kids go out for morning sports, and then they're in the common area all day. When the kids come out, the rooms are locked with automatic locks on the guards. In order to get into the room where they sleep, where they have personal bathrooms and toilets, the juvenile prisoners have to address the guard in order to enter during the day. When the guard leaves, the kids have to punch the doors for a long time to get into the bathroom. And when they ask for permission, they are subjected to insulting insults. "These are terrorists, don't talk to them." "I've been told." The four children who came with transport told them that another child who spoke to them in the air conditioning was scolded and beaten by the guards. The discussion, which began in June with verbal abuse of children in other wards for children singing a Kurdish song, resulted in children beating each other at the event where they met. Four children in prison do not want to be with other children at events and social activities for these reasons; four of them go to morning sports together, going to the library. They can't take advantage of their computer rights so that they don't come together with other children. In democratic countries, the conditions of child prisoners are based on bringing them into society and preventing their future hopes from being extinguished, while practices in our country provide the basis for children to become troubled, angry individuals in society, both in prison and out of prison.
